## Genesis 1:1 (id: 12229)

* *In the beginning, God created the heavens and the earth:* This statement summarizes the entire creation story ([1:3–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:3-Gen2:3)). It answers the key question: Who created the world? (see also [Proverbs 8:22–31](https://ref.ly/Prov8:22-Prov8:31); [John 1:1–3](https://ref.ly/John1:1-John1:3)). Genesis affirms God's role and purpose in creation.
* The common Hebrew name for God is *ʾelohim*. This name shows that God has supreme power over all things.

    The word *ʾelohim* is plural in form. However, it usually takes singular verbs. This shows that the Bible teaches there is one all\-powerful God.

* *created* (Hebrew *bara’*): In the Old Testament, when this verb is used, God is always the one creating. It describes the act of making something new, such as:

    + the heavens and the earth ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1), [21](https://ref.ly/Gen1:21); [2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen2:3)),
        + humans ([1:27](https://ref.ly/Gen1:27)),
        + the nation of Israel ([Isaiah 43:1](https://ref.ly/Isa43:1)), and
        + the future new creation ([Isaiah 65:17](https://ref.ly/Isa65:17)).
* *the heavens and the earth:* This phrase means the whole ordered universe (cosmos).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:1–2.3 (id: 12225)

* These verses introduce the Pentateuch (the first five books of the Bible). They teach Israel that one true God created, ordered, and populated the world, not the gods of nearby nations.
* God blessed three specific things:

    + animal life ([1:22–25](https://ref.ly/Gen1:22-Gen1:25)),
        + human life ([1:27](https://ref.ly/Gen1:27)), and
        + the Sabbath day ([2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen2:3)).

These three blessings emphasize the Creator's plan. God made humans in his image to rule in his place over earth's creatures and to join in God's Sabbath rest.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:1-2:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kind (ID: `keyterm:Kind.2`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Green Vegetation (ID: `flora:GreenVegetation`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`)

## Genesis 1:2 (id: 12236)

* This verse provides context for the summary in [1:1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1) and the detailed description in [1:3–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:3-Gen2:3). God's words of creation bring order to the universe's chaos.
* *formless and void* (Hebrew *tohu* and *bohu*): This phrase means something like "wild and waste." It strongly contrasts with the final ordered state of the heavens and earth ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1)).
* *surface of the deep* (Hebrew *pene tehom*): Some scholars suggest this refers to the Mesopotamian goddess Tiamat (representing chaos). But Genesis sees *tehom* as empty chaos, not as a deity or goddess that God fought in a cosmic battle.
* *the Spirit of God:* God directly guided and oversaw the process of creation.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:3 (id: 12239)

* *And God said:* God created everything in chapter [1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31) by his powerful word (compare [Psalm 33:6](https://ref.ly/Ps33:6), [9](https://ref.ly/Ps33:9)).
* *“Let there be … and there was":* God's command put his will into action to create the world. God is not part of creation or limited by it. He supremely rules over everything (see [Nehemiah 9:6](https://ref.ly/Neh9:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:3–13 (id: 12237)

In the first three days, God shaped the formless world into a place where people and animals could live.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:3-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kind (ID: `keyterm:Kind.2`); Green Vegetation (ID: `flora:GreenVegetation`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 1:4 (id: 12240)

*Light* is the opposite of chaotic *darkness* ([1:2](https://ref.ly/Gen1:2)). God considers *light to be good, but not darkness* ([John 1:5](https://ref.ly/John1:5)). God is the source of this light ([Genesis 1:14–19](https://ref.ly/Gen1:14-Gen1:19)). God separated light, just like he separated water ([1:6–8](https://ref.ly/Gen1:6-Gen1:8)), using his word that creates. Light is associated with life and blessings ([Job 38:19–20](https://ref.ly/Job38:19-Job38:20); [Psalms 19:1–6](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1-Ps19:6); [97:11](https://ref.ly/Ps97:11); [104:19–20](https://ref.ly/Ps104:19-Ps104:20); [Isaiah 60:19–20](https://ref.ly/Isa60:19-Isa60:20)). It limits the darkness that could destroy cosmic order. 

*Darkness* often represents fear, death, and evil ([Genesis 15:12](https://ref.ly/Gen15:12); [Job 18:6](https://ref.ly/Job18:6), [18](https://ref.ly/Job18:18); [Psalm 88:12](https://ref.ly/Ps88:12); [Ephesians 5:11–12](https://ref.ly/Eph5:11-Eph5:12); [1 John 1:5](https://ref.ly/1John1:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:5 (id: 12241)

* *God called* (or *named*): Naming something shows authority over it (see also [2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Gen2:19-Gen2:20)).
* *day:* The Hebrew word *yom* can mean:

    + daylight ([1:5a](https://ref.ly/Gen1:5)),
        + a 24\-hour period ([1:5b](https://ref.ly/Gen1:5)), or
        + an unspecified time ([2:4b](https://ref.ly/Gen2:4), “when,” literally *in the day;* compare [Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11)).
* *evening … morning:* The Hebrew day started at sundown. This was like the first day that began with darkness and led to morning light.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:6–8 (id: 12242)

The creation story describes how things appear from a human perspective. The sky looks like a shiny dome separating two groups of water (see [Job 37:18](https://ref.ly/Job37:18); [Ezekiel 1:22](https://ref.ly/Ezek1:22)). In the ancient Near East, people understood the universe to have three levels, with rain coming from the highest level (see [Genesis 7:11–12](https://ref.ly/Gen7:11-Gen7:12) and study note).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:6-8
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 7:11-12 (ID: `12957@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:9–10 (id: 12243)

*Let the waters …* *be gathered into one place**:* Other ancient cultures considered the sea to be a hostile force. Genesis shows God further controlling chaos by setting specific boundaries for the sea (see study note on 1:2). The flood was an act of God’s judgment ([6:7](https://ref.ly/Gen6:7)). It removed these boundaries and returned the earth to chaos ([7:1–24](https://ref.ly/Gen7:1-Gen7:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:9-10
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 1:2 (ID: `12236@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:14 (id: 12227)

*let them … mark the seasons and days and years**:* The movement of the heavenly objects ordered Israel’s liturgical calendar. This calendar, rooted in creation, provided sacred timing for Israel’s festivals and celebrations (see [Exodus 23:15](https://ref.ly/Exod23:15); [Leviticus 23:4](https://ref.ly/Lev23:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:14–31 (id: 12226)

On days four through six, God filled the domains that had been formed during days one through three ([1:3–13](https://ref.ly/Gen1:3-Gen1:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:14-31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kind (ID: `keyterm:Kind.2`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Genesis 1:16 (id: 12228)

* In the surrounding pagan cultures, people worshiped the sun and moon as gods. But in Genesis, they serve God and humanity (see [Psalm 136:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps136:7-Ps136:9); [Jeremiah 31:35](https://ref.ly/Jer31:35)).

    Here, this verse does not name the sun and moon. They are simply called *the* *greater* *light* and *the lesser* *light*. Not naming them may have reminded Israel that they were not gods.

* *rule:* Compare [1:26](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26), [28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28); [Psalm 136:9](https://ref.ly/Ps136:9).
* *the stars:* The stars bear witness to God's power to create, and they declare his glory ([Psalms 19:1](https://ref.ly/Ps19:1); [148:3](https://ref.ly/Ps148:3)). They do not predict the future, as Israel’s neighbors believed (see [Jeremiah 10:2](https://ref.ly/Jer10:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:21 (id: 12230)

Unlike pagan beliefs that the great sea creatures existed from the beginning along with God, the Bible teaches that God created them. God has complete power and authority over them. The Hebrew word *tanninim* (“sea creatures”) can also refer to:

* crocodiles ([Ezekiel 29:3](https://ref.ly/Ezek29:3)),
* powerful monsters ([Jeremiah 51:34](https://ref.ly/Jer51:34)), or
* the sea creature Leviathan ([Isaiah 27:1](https://ref.ly/Isa27:1); compare [Job 41:1–34](https://ref.ly/Job41:1-Job41:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kind (ID: `keyterm:Kind.2`); Dragon (ID: `fauna:Dragon`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:22 (id: 12231)

* *God blessed them:* This blessing orders and enables them to do what God has said (see “Blessing” Theme Note).
* *fill the waters of the seas … let the birds multiply on the earth:* These instructions explain the blessing. The fish and birds can reproduce because of God's command. It is not through pagan ceremonies, as some of Israel’s neighbors believed.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:22
* **Associated Articles:** Blessing (ID: `500163@TyndaleStudyNotesThemes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:26 (id: 12232)

* *Let Us make*: This statement is more personal than the earlier command, "Let there be" (for example, [1:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:3),[6](https://ref.ly/Gen1:6)).
* *Us*: The use of the plural pronoun here has several possible explanations:

    1. It may refer to the Trinity (one God who exists forever in three persons).
        2. It may be a plural form used to show God’s greatness.
        3. It may describe God speaking to himself (a kind of inner council).
        4. It may describe God speaking with his heavenly court (angels).
        The idea of the Trinity was revealed later in the Bible. Because of this, it is unlikely that the human author meant that here.

    Many Hebrew scholars do not accept the idea that this is a plural used to show God’s greatness. The grammar does not clearly support that view.

    The idea that God is speaking to himself also lacks clear evidence. The only similar examples refer to Israel as a group (for example, [2 Samuel 24:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:14)).

    However, the Old Testament often shows God speaking to his heavenly court (angels). This idea has strong support (see [Genesis 3:22](https://ref.ly/Gen3:22); [11:7](https://ref.ly/Gen11:7); [1 Kings 22:19–22](https://ref.ly/1Kgs22:19-1Kgs22:22); [Job 1:6–12](https://ref.ly/Job1:6-Job1:12); [2:1–6](https://ref.ly/Job2:1-Job2:6); [38:7](https://ref.ly/Job38:7); [Psalm 89:5–6](https://ref.ly/Ps89:5-Ps89:6); [Isaiah 6:1–8](https://ref.ly/Isa6:1-Isa6:8); [Daniel 10:12–13](https://ref.ly/Dan10:12-Dan10:13)).

* *in Our image, after Our likeness*: These phrases mean almost the same thing in Hebrew. Humans have a special relationship with God.
* *to rule:* Humans represent the Creator as his ambassadors and his delegated rulers on earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lizard (ID: `fauna:Lizard`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Chameleon (ID: `fauna:Chameleon`); Monitor (ID: `fauna:Iguana`)

## Genesis 1:27 (id: 12233)

* The first poetry of Genesis celebrates God's ultimate achievement in creating humans.
* *man:* This term (Hebrew *ha\-’adam*) often refers to all of humanity (see [6:1](https://ref.ly/Gen6:1), [5–7](https://ref.ly/Gen6:5-Gen6:7); [9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:5-Gen9:6)). Although traditionally translated as "man," it includes both male and female.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 1:28 (id: 12234)

* *God blessed them:* See study note on 1:22; also see [17:16](https://ref.ly/Gen17:16); [48:16](https://ref.ly/Gen48:16); [Deuteronomy 7:13](https://ref.ly/Deut7:13).
* *said:* God speaks directly and intimately to humans. We are caretakers of his shared authority.
* *subdue it; rule:* As representatives of God's rule, humans are responsible for taking care of the world God created and managing it (see also [Genesis 9:2](https://ref.ly/Gen9:2); [Psalm 8:5–8](https://ref.ly/Ps8:5-Ps8:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:28
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 1:22 (ID: `12231@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Genesis 1:29–30 (id: 12235)

These verses emphasize how God provides for humans, animals, and birds all over the earth. He gives every *seed\-bearing plant* and *every tree whose fruit contains seed*.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`)

## Genesis 1:31 (id: 12238)

The creator calls his work *good* seven times in chapter [1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31). After creating humans, God says it is all *very good*.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 1:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:1–3 (id: 12395)

Humans are the most important of God's creation ([1:26–31](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26-Gen1:31)), and day 7 is the most important of the creation week. When God rested, he approved all creation. There was nothing more needed! This seven\-day framework structured Israel's week. 

The seventh day was the model for their weekly Sabbath. God intended for the Sabbath to celebrate his completed work. God set apart the seventh day as holy. The people dedicated it to the Creator, who also rested on this day (see [Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [31:12–17](https://ref.ly/Exod31:12-Exod31:17); compare [Matthew 12:1–8](https://ref.ly/Matt12:1-Matt12:8); [Romans 14:5–6](https://ref.ly/Rom14:5-Rom14:6); [Colossians 2:16–17](https://ref.ly/Col2:16-Col2:17); [Hebrews 4:1–11](https://ref.ly/Heb4:1-Heb4:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Genesis 2:3 (id: 12410)

* During the first six days of creation, God separated light from darkness, day from night, and water from dry land. The final act of creation separated the ordinary from the holy. This was the basis for Israel’s worship. It also anticipated a future time of rest ([Hebrews 4:1–11](https://ref.ly/Heb4:1-Heb4:11); [12:2](https://ref.ly/Heb12:2); [13:14](https://ref.ly/Heb13:14)).
* The lack of the usual "evening … morning" phrase shows the creator's desire for endless fellowship with humans.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Genesis 2:4 (id: 12412)

* *This is the account* (literally *These are the generations*; Hebrew *toledoth*): This phrase appears often in Genesis. It helps outline the book. It usually introduces the family line or story of an important person ([5:1](https://ref.ly/Gen5:1); [6:9](https://ref.ly/Gen6:9); [10:1](https://ref.ly/Gen10:1); [11:10](https://ref.ly/Gen11:10), [27](https://ref.ly/Gen11:27); [25:12](https://ref.ly/Gen25:12), [19](https://ref.ly/Gen25:19); [36:1](https://ref.ly/Gen36:1); [37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2)).

    Some people think the first part of [2:4](https://ref.ly/Gen2:4) belongs with [1:1–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen2:3), but it probably introduces the next story.

* *LORD God* (Hebrew *Yahweh Elohim*): This is the second name for God in Genesis. *Elohim* ([1:1–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen2:3)) refers to the all\-powerful creator God. *Yahweh Elohim* refers to the eternal God who made a lasting covenant with Israel ([Exodus 3:6](https://ref.ly/Exod3:6), [13–15](https://ref.ly/Exod3:13-Exod3:15)). So, [Genesis 2:4–25](https://ref.ly/Gen2:4-Gen2:25) focuses on God as a provider more than as a creator.

    The three themes of sexuality, dominion, and food in chapter [1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31) are now in reverse order (food, [2:8–17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:8-Gen2:17); dominion, [2:18–20](https://ref.ly/Gen2:18-Gen2:20); sexuality, [2:21–25](https://ref.ly/Gen2:21-Gen2:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:4–25 (id: 12411)

This story (see study note on 2:4) of the heavens and the earth is not a second creation story. Instead, it expands on [1:1–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen2:3) with more theological and historical details. The focus shifts to what the universe (*kosmos*) produced rather than on its creation. 

Special attention is given to the first man and woman. As the story continues, it is shaped by contrasts such as good and evil, knowledge and ignorance, life and death, and harmony and conflict.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:4-25
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:4 (ID: `12412@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah`); Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush`); Gihon (ID: `place:Gihon`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 2:5 (id: 12413)

Work does not result from sin. It was part of creation's original design. It has a close link to human identity and purpose ([1:28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28); [2:15](https://ref.ly/Gen2:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 2:6 (id: 12414)

*springs:* The word refers to underground water that rises up to the surface of the ground.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:6


## Genesis 2:7 (id: 12415)

* In [1:1–2:3](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen2:3), creation happens by God's command ("Let there be ... And it was so"). This story describes a more personal process of creation (see also [2:8–9](https://ref.ly/Gen2:8-Gen2:9), [21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen2:21-Gen2:22)).
* *from the dust of the ground:* In Hebrew, *'adamah* means "ground*.*" This forms a wordplay on *'adam,* whichmeans "man." The earth is a key reference point for humans, who become dust again at death ([3:17–19](https://ref.ly/Gen3:17-Gen3:19); [4:11](https://ref.ly/Gen4:11); [Job 4:19](https://ref.ly/Job4:19); [10:8–9](https://ref.ly/Job10:8-Job10:9); [Isaiah 29:16](https://ref.ly/Isa29:16)).
* *breathed the breath of life into his nostrils:* God gives his breath only to humans, not to other animals. God made humans in his image ([Genesis 1:27](https://ref.ly/Gen1:27)) and only they can communicate with their Creator ([2:16–17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:16-Gen2:17); [3:8–13](https://ref.ly/Gen3:8-Gen3:13)). They are the only ones with spiritual awareness and a moral conscience (see [Job 32:8](https://ref.ly/Job32:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:8 (id: 12417)

* Eden was the general location in which the garden was placed, not the garden itself. The term could mean "plain," "delight," or "fertility." The description that follows suggests the idea of fertility.
* *in the east:* The exact location of Eden is unknown. But it was east of Canaan, which later became Israel's home.
* God placed the man in the garden to have a relationship with him and to receive his physical blessings (see also [2:15](https://ref.ly/Gen2:15) and study note there).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:8
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:15 (ID: `12402@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:8–14 (id: 12416)

Similar to the holy time set on the seventh day of creation ([2:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen2:2-Gen2:3)), the garden in Eden was a holy place separate from the rest of the world. It was like a garden\-temple or sanctuary because the Lord showed his presence there in a special way.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:8-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush`); Gihon (ID: `place:Gihon`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Genesis 2:9 (id: 12418)

* Beauty and abundance characterized humanity's original environment (see [13:10](https://ref.ly/Gen13:10)).
* *the tree of life*: This represented God's presence and care. Eating from it would give everlasting life ([3:22](https://ref.ly/Gen3:22)), making it a powerful image for later Israelite and Christian thought ([Proverbs 3:18](https://ref.ly/Prov3:18); [11:30](https://ref.ly/Prov11:30); [13:12](https://ref.ly/Prov13:12); [Revelation 2:7](https://ref.ly/Rev2:7); [22:2](https://ref.ly/Rev22:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Rev22:14), [19](https://ref.ly/Rev22:19)).

    The candlestick in Israel’s tabernacle may have been a stylized representation of the tree of life ([Exodus 25:31–35](https://ref.ly/Exod25:31-Exod25:35)).

* Eating the fruit from *the tree of the knowledge of good and evil* gave humans the ability to be wise ([Genesis 3:6](https://ref.ly/Gen3:6)) and have moral understanding ([3:5](https://ref.ly/Gen3:5), [22](https://ref.ly/Gen3:22); compare [Deuteronomy 1:39](https://ref.ly/Deut1:39), “not know good from evil”).

    Eating from it showed a human desire to be independent and have wisdom that belonged to God alone (see [Proverbs 30:1–4](https://ref.ly/Prov30:1-Prov30:4)). Humans ignored God's instruction for moral understanding, showing independence instead of following God's will (compare [Proverbs 1:7](https://ref.ly/Prov1:7)). Choosing human wisdom over God's instruction brings death and destruction (see [Psalm 19:7–9](https://ref.ly/Ps19:7-Ps19:9); [Ezekiel 28:6](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:6), [15–17](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:15-Ezek28:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Genesis 2:10 (id: 12397)

* The river that watered the garden was a physical blessing, bringing fertile land for farming. It also represented God's presence (compare [Psalm 46:4](https://ref.ly/Ps46:4); [Ezekiel 47:1–12](https://ref.ly/Ezek47:1-Ezek47:12); [Zechariah 14:8](https://ref.ly/Zech14:8); [Revelation 22:1–2](https://ref.ly/Rev22:1-Rev22:2)).
* *branched into four headwaters*: The common understanding is that one river had its source in Eden, flowed down through the garden, and then split into the four rivers named.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`)

## Genesis 2:10–14 (id: 12396)

This description portrays the eastern region around Eden as a mountain with rivers flowing out to the world. Eden's beauty and fertility enriched the entire earth.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:10-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush`); Gihon (ID: `place:Gihon`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`)

## Genesis 2:11 (id: 12398)

The Pishon and the Gihon ([2:13](https://ref.ly/Gen2:13)) cannot be identified with certainty. If the land of Havilah was in southeast Arabia or on the African coast, as some biblical data suggest (see [10:7](https://ref.ly/Gen10:7); [25:18](https://ref.ly/Gen25:18); [1 Samuel 15:7](https://ref.ly/1Sam15:7)), then the Pishon was possibly the Nile River. The Jewish historian Josephus thought that Havilah and the Pishon were in India (Antiquities 1\.1\.3\). 

Two other ideas suggest the Pishon and Gihon might be: 

1. rivers in the mountains of eastern Turkey, where the Tigris and Euphrates ([Genesis 2:14](https://ref.ly/Gen2:14)) also flow, and
2. the marshy delta near the Persian Gulf.

Current geographical conditions make it impossible to prove any theory with certainty.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Havilah (ID: `place:Havilah`)

## Genesis 2:12 (id: 12399)

* The garden's beauty and richness spread to nearby areas through its rivers. The four rivers might suggest the garden's bounty reached all parts of the world.
* The Israeliteslater used *gold* and *onyx* to decorate the tabernacle, the temple ([Exodus 25:3–9](https://ref.ly/Exod25:3-Exod25:9); [1 Chronicles 29:2](https://ref.ly/1Chr29:2)), and the priests' clothing ([Exodus 28:9–14](https://ref.ly/Exod28:9-Exod28:14), [20](https://ref.ly/Exod28:20)).
* The people used *bdellium* (a fragrant resin) in sacred incense ([Exodus 30:34](https://ref.ly/Exod30:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:12


## Genesis 2:13 (id: 12400)

* *Gihon:* The exact location is unknown, but some think it could be the Nile River or the Jordan River (as in the Greek version of [Jeremiah 2:18](https://ref.ly/Jer2:18); Josephus, *Antiquities* 1\.1\.3\). Or according to Jewish tradition, it could be a river that once flowed through the Kidron Valley ([1 Kings 1:33](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:33); [2 Chronicles 33:14](https://ref.ly/2Chr33:14)).
* Although Cush often refers to ancient Ethiopia, the immediate setting here seems to be a region in Mesopotamia connected with Babylon (see [10:8](https://ref.ly/Gen10:8)). Cush may refer to the land of the Kassites, a people who ruled in Babylonia.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cush (ID: `place:Cush`); Gihon (ID: `place:Gihon`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`)

## Genesis 2:14 (id: 12401)

*Tigris … Euphrates:* These well\-known rivers flow from the mountains of eastern Turkey.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tigris River (ID: `place:Tigris`); Euphrates River (ID: `place:Euphrates`); Assyria (ID: `place:Assyria`)

## Genesis 2:15 (id: 12402)

*to cultivate and keep it:* The garden needed care and management. Caring for the garden was humanity's honorable work. The Scriptures later apply these roles in God's garden\-sanctuary to his tabernacle (see [Leviticus 8:35](https://ref.ly/Lev8:35); [Numbers 3:5–10](https://ref.ly/Num3:5-Num3:10); [4:46–49](https://ref.ly/Num4:46-Num4:49)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:17 (id: 12403)

* *but you must not eat*: This rule is given in the same way as Israel’s Ten Commandments (see [Exodus 20:1–17](https://ref.ly/Exod20:1-Exod20:17); [Deuteronomy 5:6–21](https://ref.ly/Deut5:6-Deut5:21)). The Lord included law and obedience in his covenant with humanity.
* *the knowledge of good and evil:* See study note on 2:9.
* *you will surely die:* Disobedience would bring immediate spiritual death (loss of relationship with God) and eventual physical death (see [3:22–23](https://ref.ly/Gen3:22-Gen3:23); [Ecclesiastes 12:6–7](https://ref.ly/Eccl12:6-Eccl12:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:17
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:9 (ID: `12418@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`)

## Genesis 2:18 (id: 12405)

* *It is not good:* This is God's first negative comment about an otherwise good creation ([1:31](https://ref.ly/Gen1:31)). The Lord God is like a father finding a bride for his son (compare chapter [24](https://ref.ly/Gen24:1-Gen24:67)).
* The solution for the man's need is a helper who is perfect for him. She is his ideal partner. God made her in the same image of God ([1:26–27](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26-Gen1:27)), gave her the same mission ([1:28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28); [2:15](https://ref.ly/Gen2:15)), and gave her the same rule ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17)). The man cannot achieve his purpose alone.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 2:18–23 (id: 12404)

Human creation was the most important part of chapter [1](https://ref.ly/Gen1:1-Gen1:31), and human intimacy is the highlight of chapter [2](https://ref.ly/Gen2:1-Gen2:25). God's focus on mutual support and companionship among humans is unique in ancient Near Eastern writings.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:18-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 2:19–20 (id: 12406)

*to see what he would name each one**:* Following God's example ([1:5](https://ref.ly/Gen1:5), [8](https://ref.ly/Gen1:8), [10](https://ref.ly/Gen1:10)), the man named each creature. By doing this, he showed his authority over creation ([1:26](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26), [28](https://ref.ly/Gen1:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 2:23 (id: 12407)

* Adam recognized the woman as his "suitable helper" ([2:20](https://ref.ly/Gen2:20)). He celebrated their unity through poetry and song, focusing on their connection, not their differences (compare [29:14](https://ref.ly/Gen29:14)).
* "*she shall be called ‘woman,’ for out of man she was taken":* Adam understood their connection (see [Ephesians 5:28–29](https://ref.ly/Eph5:28-Eph5:29)). Earlier, Adam looked at the animals but did not find a suitable partner. This evaluation is very different!

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`)

## Genesis 2:24 (id: 12408)

* Marriage between a man and a woman is not only a human idea. It comes from the natural order.
* *a man will leave his father and mother and be united to his wife:* Marriage involves shifting loyalty from parents to a spouse.
* *they will become one flesh*: Marriage and its commitments make it the most basic covenant among people. Marriage is a powerful image of Israel’s covenant with God ([Hosea 2:14–23](https://ref.ly/Hos2:14-Hos2:23)) and of Christ’s relationship to the Church ([Ephesians 5:22–32](https://ref.ly/Eph5:22-Eph5:32)). God designed marriage to be a permanent, exclusive relationship between a man and a woman. The family it creates is the foundation of human society.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:24


## Genesis 2:25 (id: 12409)

*both naked:* Before the first humans disobeyed God (chapter [3](https://ref.ly/Gen3:1-Gen3:24)), being naked showed innocence and trust. After this, it came to show vulnerability and shame (see [9:22–23](https://ref.ly/Gen9:22-Gen9:23); [Leviticus 18:1–23](https://ref.ly/Lev18:1-Lev18:23); [Isaiah 47:3](https://ref.ly/Isa47:3)). Shame is more than embarrassment. It can include being exposed, mistreated, or humiliated (see [Deuteronomy 28:48](https://ref.ly/Deut28:48); [Isaiah 58:7](https://ref.ly/Isa58:7); [James 2:15–16](https://ref.ly/Jas2:15-Jas2:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 2:25


## Genesis 3:1 (id: 12598)

* Genesis describes the deceiver as a serpent, one of the animals God created (see also [3:14](https://ref.ly/Gen3:14) and study note). Scripture later identifies him as Satan, the great enemy of God’s people ([Revelation 12:9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:9); [20:2](https://ref.ly/Rev20:2)). He used manipulative language and disguised himself as a serpent. The serpent was the most clever of all creatures. This showed Satan as a master deceiver.

    Satan uses different methods to oppose God’s people (see [1 Chronicles 21:1](https://ref.ly/1Chr21:1); [Zechariah 3:1–2](https://ref.ly/Zech3:1-Zech3:2)). Deception remains one of his main strategies (compare [2 Corinthians 11:3](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:3), [14](https://ref.ly/2Cor11:14)).

    The Hebrew word for *crafty* is *‘arum.* It can have a positive meaning (“prudent,” [Proverbs 14:8](https://ref.ly/Prov14:8)) or a negative meaning (as here; see [Job 5:12](https://ref.ly/Job5:12)). Here it forms a wordplay with “naked” (Hebrew *‘arummim*) in [Genesis 2:25](https://ref.ly/Gen2:25). Adam and Eve were naked and vulnerable. The serpent was crafty and cunning.

* The serpent probably asked the woman because God gave Adam the rule before he created Eve (see [2:16–17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:16-Gen2:17)). Adam was probably aware of the serpent’s craftiness, since he had named all the animals before God created Eve ([2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Gen2:19-Gen2:20), [23](https://ref.ly/Gen2:23)).
* *Did God really say:* The deceiver started by twisting God’s words to make the people doubt God’s goodness. God’s original rule applied to only one tree ([2:16–17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:16-Gen2:17)), not to all of them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:1
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:14 (ID: `12594@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Crafty (ID: `keyterm:Crafty`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 3:1–24 (id: 12590)

When the man and woman rebelled against God, it disrupted their harmony with the earth, animals, each other, and God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:1-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Eve (ID: `person:Eve`); Crafty (ID: `keyterm:Crafty`); Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 3:2–3 (id: 12599)

The woman tried to clarify things. But in several ways, she made the privileges God gave her and her husband seem less important:

1. She changed God’s words from “freely eat” ([2:16](https://ref.ly/Gen2:16)) to *may eat*.
2. She minimized the importance of having fruit from every tree except one ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17)).
3. She added not touching to God’s rule against eating ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17)).
4. She made the threat of death seem less certain ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Genesis 3:4–5 (id: 12606)

*You will not surely die:* This directly contradicts God's clear and emphatic words: “you will surely die” ([2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17)). The serpent takes advantage of the woman's doubt. He denies there will be any penalty and quickly shifts her focus to the supposed reward. He says she will become like God, knowing good and evil. 

The deceiver falsely implies that this would be totally beneficial for them. The term used for God is *Elohim*. It can also mean “divine beings,” such as God and the angels (for example, [Psalms 29:1](https://ref.ly/Ps29:1); [89:7](https://ref.ly/Ps89:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Genesis 3:6 (id: 12607)

* *She saw … that it was desirable:* The woman made two serious mistakes:

    1. She assumed she could decide what was good or bad. But God is the only one who has the right to decide what is good or bad.
        2. She coveted God's wisdom, meaning she strongly desired something that did not belong to her (see [Deuteronomy 5:21](https://ref.ly/Deut5:21)).
* *her husband who was with her:* Scripture highlights the woman's role in humanity's first sin (compare [1 Timothy 2:14](https://ref.ly/1Tim2:14)). But, the man was also present and responsible. He becomes the focus in the following verses and in biblical teaching. His sin brought serious consequences for all people. The good news is that through Jesus Christ, the “second Adam,” God offers salvation to everyone ([Romans 5:12–21](https://ref.ly/Rom5:12-Rom5:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Have Insight (ID: `keyterm:HaveInsight`)

## Genesis 3:7 (id: 12608)

* Shame is the opposite of the naked innocence Adam and Eve enjoyed before they disobeyed God ([2:25](https://ref.ly/Gen2:25)). This damaged their relationship with each other and God.
* *sewed together fig leaves:* These covered their bodies but not their shame. They could not fix their broken relationships (see also [3:21](https://ref.ly/Gen3:21) and study note).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:7
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:21 (ID: `12602@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fig (ID: `flora:Fig`)

## Genesis 3:8 (id: 12609)

* *in the garden in the breeze of the day*: Traditionally, the Hebrew text refers to the cool part of the day, probably the evening. Some believe it describes a powerful appearance of God's presence (a *theophany*; see [Exodus 19:16–25](https://ref.ly/Exod19:16-Exod19:25); [1 Samuel 7:10](https://ref.ly/1Sam7:10)) as a storm. If this is true, the man and woman hid from the sound of the Lord appearing in judgment (see [2 Samuel 5:24](https://ref.ly/2Sam5:24); [Psalm 29](https://ref.ly/Ps29:1-Ps29:11)).
* God placed *the trees of the garden* for humans to enjoy fellowship with him. Now, the man and woman used them to hide from God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 3:9–10 (id: 12610)

* *Where are you?:* The man's answer shows the real purpose of this question ([3:10](https://ref.ly/Gen3:10)). The real meaning of the question was, "Why are you hiding?" (compare [4:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen4:9-Gen4:10)).
* *I was afraid because I was naked:* Modesty was not the problem. The shame from their rebellion made Adam and his wife hide. They might have also feared punishment (see study note on 3:8).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:9-10
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:8 (ID: `12609@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 3:12 (id: 12591)

*The woman whom You gave me:* The man avoided admitting his sin. He blamed the woman for giving him the fruit and blamed God for giving him the woman.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:12


## Genesis 3:13 (id: 12592)

* *What is this you have done?:* This question is not asked to get information. It is an expression of shock and horror (see [4:10](https://ref.ly/Gen4:10)).
* *The serpent deceived me:* The man blamed the woman ([3:12](https://ref.ly/Gen3:12)), and the woman blamed the serpent. The serpent did play a role and would be punished ([3:14](https://ref.ly/Gen3:14)). But this did not free the woman or the man from their guilt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Genesis 3:14 (id: 12594)

* *to the serpent:* Though later revelation identifies the deceiver as Satan, it is the created animal who was cursed, like the ground ([3:17](https://ref.ly/Gen3:17)).
* *On your belly will you go, and dust you will eat:* Groveling in the dust shows humiliation and defeat ([Psalm 72:9](https://ref.ly/Ps72:9); [Micah 7:17](https://ref.ly/Mic7:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`)

## Genesis 3:14–19 (id: 12593)

God judged the serpent, woman, and man because of their sin. Each received a punishment specific to their actions. Their important relationships changed. God is fair in judgment, not unpredictable. Each punishment matches the sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:14-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Snake (ID: `fauna:Snake`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 3:15 (id: 12595)

* *enmity:* The prophet Isaiah looks ahead to a future when the Messiah's kingdom will restore all creation to peace, like the garden of Eden before humans disobeyed God (see [Isaiah 11:8](https://ref.ly/Isa11:8)).
* *her seed* (or *offspring*)*:* This term can refer to a single descendant or many. In ancient Near Eastern culture, "you and your offspring" (see [Genesis 28:14](https://ref.ly/Gen28:14)) describes a shared identity. Here, it describes ongoing hostility between humans and snakes.

    The pattern is set using singular terms (*He … you*). Christian interpreters have traditionally understood this verse to be a prophecy about Christ. He is the seed of Abraham and fulfills the promise of the woman's seed (see [Galatians 3:16](https://ref.ly/Gal3:16); [4:4](https://ref.ly/Gal4:4)).

* *strike:* This refers to the suffering of God's servant (see [Isaiah 53](https://ref.ly/Isa53:1-Isa53:12)). Crushing the serpent's head is a more decisive action. The death, resurrection, and final victory of Jesus over Satan fulfill this promise (see [1 Corinthians 15:55–57](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:55-1Cor15:57); [Revelation 12:7–9](https://ref.ly/Rev12:7-Rev12:9); [20:7–10](https://ref.ly/Rev20:7-Rev20:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:15


## Genesis 3:16 (id: 12596)

* The judgment affects the woman's special role in childbearing and her relationship with her husband.
* *Your desire will be for your husband, and he will rule over you:* The marriage relationship now includes conflict instead of only security and happiness. New life in Jesus can restore a man and woman's marriage relationship ([Ephesians 5:18–32](https://ref.ly/Eph5:18-Eph5:32); compare [Matthew 20:25–28](https://ref.ly/Matt20:25-Matt20:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:16


## Genesis 3:17–19 (id: 12597)

* God emphasized his first command not to eat the fruit by mentioning eating several times in [3:17–19](https://ref.ly/Gen3:17-Gen3:19). The judgment made it harder for humans to get food. This matched their sin of eating what God had forbidden.
* *cursed is the ground:* The man's relationship with the ground (see study note on 2:7) became hostile as judgment affected his main role ([2:5](https://ref.ly/Gen2:5), [15](https://ref.ly/Gen2:15)). He must work hard to cultivate the ground, but with less success than before. Human sin impacts all of creation (see [4:12](https://ref.ly/Gen4:12); [6:7](https://ref.ly/Gen6:7); [Leviticus 26](https://ref.ly/Lev26:1-Lev26:46); [Deuteronomy 11:13–17](https://ref.ly/Deut11:13-Deut11:17), [28](https://ref.ly/Deut11:28); [Romans 8:22](https://ref.ly/Rom8:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:17-19
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:7 (ID: `12415@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`)

## Genesis 3:20 (id: 12601)

The name *Eve* (Hebrew *khawah*) sounds like the word *khayah*, which means "to give life." After God announced Adam's future death ([3:19](https://ref.ly/Gen3:19)), Adam showed hope by naming Eve with a name related to life. Adam named Eve soon after [3:16](https://ref.ly/Gen3:16). Some understand this to show that Adam’s act of naming the woman reflects his role and responsibility in the relationship after they sinned (see study note on 2:19–20).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:20
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:19-20 (ID: `12406@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Eve (ID: `person:Eve`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`)

## Genesis 3:20–24 (id: 12600)

Soon after they were judged for their sin, Adam and Eve were banished from the garden.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:20-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Eve (ID: `person:Eve`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`)

## Genesis 3:21 (id: 12602)

God showed mercy and gave Adam and Eve better clothing (compare [3:7](https://ref.ly/Gen3:7)) before sending them into the harsh world outside the garden.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic.2`)

## Genesis 3:22 (id: 12603)

* *like one of Us:* The plural likely shows God speaking with his heavenly court of angels (see study note on 1:26).
* *the tree of life … live forever:* God showed mercy by preventing the humans from eating from the tree of life and living forever in a sinful condition. But through Jesus Christ, eternal life is available again (see [Revelation 2:7](https://ref.ly/Rev2:7); [22:2](https://ref.ly/Rev22:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Rev22:14), [19](https://ref.ly/Rev22:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 1:26 (ID: `12232@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 3:23 (id: 12604)

*Therefore* *the LORD God banished him from the Garden of Eden:* Before humans disobeyed God, the garden was a sanctuary where humans could be in God's holy presence. Now their sin meant God had to expel them from that place. This same idea also influenced Israel's laws. The laws limited the access Israelites had to God in the tabernacle or temple (for example, [Leviticus 16:1–2](https://ref.ly/Lev16:1-Lev16:2); [Numbers 5:3](https://ref.ly/Num5:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 3:24 (id: 12605)

* Cherubim are a type of angel that guard access to God's presence ([Exodus 26:31](https://ref.ly/Exod26:31); [Ezekiel 28:14](https://ref.ly/Ezek28:14)).
* *east … of Eden:* In Genesis, moving east often means leaving God's presence or blessing. This can happen because of:

    + judgment ([Genesis 4:16](https://ref.ly/Gen4:16)),
        + human pride or independence ([Genesis 11:2](https://ref.ly/Gen11:2); [Genesis 13:11](https://ref.ly/Gen13:11)), or
        + separation from the family ([Genesis 25:6](https://ref.ly/Gen25:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 3:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); Winged Creature (ID: `realia:WingedCreature`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Genesis 4:1 (id: 12775)

* *had relations* (literally *knew*): In some contexts, the Hebrew word meaning "to know" is a way to describe having sexual relations with someone ([4:17](https://ref.ly/Gen4:17); [19:33](https://ref.ly/Gen19:33), [35](https://ref.ly/Gen19:35)). It is never used for animals, which mate by instinct.
* *With the help of the LORD:* Eve fulfilled her God\-given role of having children despite the negative effects of sin (see [3:16](https://ref.ly/Gen3:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Gen3:20)).
* *I have brought forth a man:* *Cain* (Hebrew *qayin*) sounds like a Hebrew word (*qanah*) that can mean “produce” or “acquire.”

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Eve (ID: `person:Eve`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 4:2 (id: 12781)

*Cain's brother Abel:* The name "Abel" may come from the Hebrew word *habel,* meaning "breath," "vapor," or "meaningless." This might hint at Abel's tragically short life (compare [Ecclesiastes 1:2](https://ref.ly/Eccl1:2)). Alternatively, it may come from the Akkadian word *ablu*, meaning "son."

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 4:3 (id: 12782)

There was nothing wrong with offering grain to the Lord ([Leviticus 2:14](https://ref.ly/Lev2:14); [Deuteronomy 26:2–4](https://ref.ly/Deut26:2-Deut26:4)). But Cain only gave a small portion of his crops. God requires the first and best ([Exodus 23:16](https://ref.ly/Exod23:16), [19](https://ref.ly/Exod23:19); [34:22](https://ref.ly/Exod34:22), [26](https://ref.ly/Exod34:26)). Cain's attitude made his offering less valuable than Abel's ([Hebrews 11:4](https://ref.ly/Heb11:4)). So, God did not accept Cain's offering.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Sorghum (ID: `flora:Sorghum`)

## Genesis 4:4–5 (id: 12783)

*the best portions of the firstborn of his flock* (*o*r *the firstborn of his flock and their fat portions*): Abel gave God the best animals and their richest parts. Unlike Cain, Abel offered the best he had. True worship is a costly privilege.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:4-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 4:7 (id: 12784)

*sin is crouching at your door … you must master it**:* Sin is like a wild animal ready to attack Cain (see study note on 3:16). Either sin will control Cain, or Cain will fight against the urge to sin. There is no middle ground in this struggle.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:7
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:16 (ID: `12596@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Genesis 4:8 (id: 12785)

* The impact of the sin on human relationships is tragically shown in the first murder.
* The word *brother* appears seven times in [4:2–11](https://ref.ly/Gen4:2-Gen4:11), emphasizing Cain's act of killing his brother despite responsibility to one's family.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`)

## Genesis 4:9 (id: 12786)

*Where is your brother?:* The questions God asked Cain ([4:6](https://ref.ly/Gen4:6), [9](https://ref.ly/Gen4:9), [10](https://ref.ly/Gen4:10)) remind us of those God asked Cain’s parents ([3:9–13](https://ref.ly/Gen3:9-Gen3:13)). In both situations, humans answered by avoiding the truth (compare [3:12–13](https://ref.ly/Gen3:12-Gen3:13)). It is surprising how defiant Cain’s answer is. This suggests that his attitude was the real problem with his offering.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 4:10 (id: 12766)

* *What have you done?:* This is more an expression of horror and blame than a question seeking answers (see [3:13](https://ref.ly/Gen3:13)).
* Abel’s blood acts as a legal witness that accuses Cain of a crime.
* *from the ground:* See study note on 4:11–12.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:10
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 4:11-12 (ID: `12767@TyndaleStudyNotes`)


## Genesis 4:11–12 (id: 12767)

* Like his father (see [3:9–12](https://ref.ly/Gen3:9-Gen3:12), [17–19](https://ref.ly/Gen3:17-Gen3:19)), God asked Cain questions before he judged him ([4:9–10](https://ref.ly/Gen4:9-Gen4:10)). Adam's sin had already caused the ground to be cursed. Now Cain was cursed and driven away from the land he had farmed because he had polluted it with innocent blood.
* *a fugitive and a wanderer:* God sentenced Cain to endless wandering in a land that offered neither food nor safety. The consequences of sin were becoming worse.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`)

## Genesis 4:13–14 (id: 12768)

For Cain, being driven from the land (the place where he worked as a farmer; see [4:2](https://ref.ly/Gen4:2); compare [3:23](https://ref.ly/Gen3:23)) meant being separated from God’s presence. God warned the Israelites to remain faithful to the Sinai covenant. If not, he would force them to leave the promised land. This would also mean separation from God's presence in the temple (see, for example, [Leviticus 26:27–32](https://ref.ly/Lev26:27-Lev26:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:13-14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bear the Consequences Of (ID: `keyterm:BearTheConsequencesOf`)

## Genesis 4:15 (id: 12769)

* Sevenfold punishment was the full weight of justice. Cain complained that his punishment was too harsh. But the severe sentence for anyone who killed Cain shows how gracious the Lord was to him. Cain deserved death (see [9:5–6](https://ref.ly/Gen9:5-Gen9:6)).
* The *mark* graciously provided protection to Cain after God's judgment (compare [3:21](https://ref.ly/Gen3:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 4:16 (id: 12770)

* The name *Nod* (which means "wandering") describes Cain's fate (see [4:12](https://ref.ly/Gen4:12), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen4:14)) more than a specific place. It suggests a life of wandering, and its exact location is unknown. Cain’s sin denied him rest and a sense of belonging.
* Cain’s exile east of Eden is another point of connection with Adam’s story (compare [3:24](https://ref.ly/Gen3:24)). Cain did not learn from his father’s sin, so he also experienced separation from the ground and was driven east into exile (see study note on 3:24).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:16
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 3:24 (ID: `12605@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nod (ID: `place:Nod`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Garden of Eden (ID: `place:Eden.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 4:17 (id: 12772)

* Cain likely married a close relative, since Adam and Eve had other sons and daughters ([5:4](https://ref.ly/Gen5:4)). The Bible does not give further details.
* God condemned Cain to be a wanderer. He might have built a city to rebel against this punishment, using walls for protection. Naming the city after his son shows how people who rebel against God often make humanity and its achievements into idols.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Enoch (ID: `person:Enoch`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`)

## Genesis 4:17–5.32 (id: 12771)

These records of family lines do more than just list names. They contrast how human culture developed. Some people rebelled against God (Cain, [4:17–24](https://ref.ly/Gen4:17-Gen4:24)), while others obeyed God (Seth, [4:25–5:32](https://ref.ly/Gen4:25-Gen5:32)). Genesis usually explains the history of the rejected line before continuing with the promised line that became Israel. 

Two key contrasts are worth noting: 

1. Lamech is the main focus of the first family history. He is the seventh man from Adam through Cain's line. Like Cain, Lamech killed a human and lived in fear of death as a result ([4:23–24](https://ref.ly/Gen4:23-Gen4:24)). In contrast, Enoch lived in a way that pleased God and did not experience death ([5:24](https://ref.ly/Gen5:24)). Enoch was the seventh man from Adam through Seth's line ([4:25–5:32](https://ref.ly/Gen4:25-Gen5:32)).
2. Human culture and technology advanced through Cain’s line. They made the first city, raised livestock, built shelters, developed metal work, and created music. But sin still strongly shaped human life. Seth's line does not have any technological advances mentioned. Instead, people began "to call upon the name of the LORD" ([4:26](https://ref.ly/Gen4:26)) and "found favor in the eyes of the Lord" ([6:8](https://ref.ly/Gen6:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:17-5:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Enosh (ID: `person:Enosh`); Seth (ID: `person:Seth`); Enoch (ID: `person:Enoch.2`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Lamech (ID: `person:Lamech.2`); Methuselah (ID: `person:Methuselah`); Jared (ID: `person:Jared`); Mahalalel (ID: `person:Mahalalel`); Kenan (ID: `person:Kenan`); Lamech (ID: `person:Lamech`); Zillah (ID: `person:Zillah`); Adah (ID: `person:Adah`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`); Enoch (ID: `person:Enoch`); Jubal (ID: `person:Jubal`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`); Irad (ID: `person:Irad`); Mehujael (ID: `person:Mehujael`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Methushael (ID: `person:Methushael`); Tubal-cain (ID: `person:Tubal-cain`); Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Jabal (ID: `person:Jabal`); Naamah (ID: `person:Naamah.2`); Abel (ID: `person:Abel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Lute (ID: `realia:Lute`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Flute (ID: `realia:Flute`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

*father of*: This can also mean “ancestor of,” and this meaning applies throughout the verse. Hebrew family lists do not always include every generation.

## Genesis 4:19 (id: 12774)

Marrying two women was against God’s ideal pattern for marriage ([2:24](https://ref.ly/Gen2:24)). It might also show the pride and rebellion of Cain's descendants.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lamech (ID: `person:Lamech`); Adah (ID: `person:Adah`); Zillah (ID: `person:Zillah`)

## Genesis 4:20–22 (id: 12776)

New tools and skills can hide a growing desire for people to rely on themselves instead of God. They can also show that people are moving farther away from God (see study note on 4:17–5:32).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:20-22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 4:17-5.32 (ID: `12771@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 4:23–24 (id: 12777)

* Lamech's disturbing boast shows how sin's impact on humanity has increased. Cain's descendants reached an extreme of violence with Lamech's contempt for human life. In his arrogance, he turned his actions into a poem.
* *avenged … seventy\-sevenfold:* God warned that anyone who tried to kill Cain would experience total justice ([4:15](https://ref.ly/Gen4:15)). Lamech claimed that anyone who harmed him would face an even more severe penalty. This shows he felt accountable to no one, not even God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Lamech (ID: `person:Lamech`); Adah (ID: `person:Adah`); Zillah (ID: `person:Zillah`); Cain (ID: `person:Cain`)

## Genesis 4:25 (id: 12779)

*another seed in place of Abel:* Cain ([4:8–16](https://ref.ly/Gen4:8-Gen4:16)) and Lamech ([4:19–24](https://ref.ly/Gen4:19-Gen4:24)) show the consequences of sin. The birth of Seth brought new hope. See also study note on 5:1–2.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:25

The story returns to Adam and follows the line of Seth. This family line later leads to Abraham and the nation of Israel.

## Genesis 4:26 (id: 12780)

*Enosh* means "humankind." In the Old Testament, this term often appears in poems emphasizing human mortality and weakness (for example, [Psalm 144:3](https://ref.ly/Ps144:3), "what is man"). 

Enosh was born when people started to worship the Lord by name (literally *call upon the name of the* *LORD*). In Genesis, this meant worshiping the Lord through sacrifice and prayer (similar Hebrew terms are found in [Genesis 12:8](https://ref.ly/Gen12:8); [13:4](https://ref.ly/Gen13:4); [21:33](https://ref.ly/Gen21:33); [26:25](https://ref.ly/Gen26:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 4:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Enosh (ID: `person:Enosh`); Seth (ID: `person:Seth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 5:1 (id: 12920)

* *the book of the generations:* The earlier section ([2:4–4:26](https://ref.ly/Gen2:4-Gen4:26)) focused on Adam, Eve, and their first children. But it was more about "the account of the heavens and the earth." [Genesis 5:1–32](https://ref.ly/Gen5:1-Gen5:32) is a more typical family line.
* *in His own likeness:* See [1:26](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26) and study note.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 5:1
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 1:26 (ID: `12232@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 5:1–2 (id: 12918)

This section introduces the second account in Genesis ([5:1–6:8](https://ref.ly/Gen5:1-Gen6:8); see study note on 2:4). It connects God’s purpose in creation with the line of Seth rather than the line of Cain ([4:17–24](https://ref.ly/Gen4:17-Gen4:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 5:1-2
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 2:4 (ID: `12412@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 5:1–32 (id: 12919)

The family lines in Genesis do more than record history. They use select details and structure to communicate spiritual truths. These genealogies show God's blessings. They confirm the family heritage of key people. They connect the Genesis story by showing family continuity. 

Adam's family line through Seth records ten generations to Noah (see [1 Chronicles 1:1–4](https://ref.ly/1Chr1:1-1Chr1:4); [Luke 3:36–38](https://ref.ly/Luke3:36-Luke3:38)). The great flood occurred before another 10 generations from Noah to Abram. The number 10 symbolizes completeness. Examples include the 10 plagues ([Exodus 7:8–11:10](https://ref.ly/Exod7:8-Exod11:10)) and the Ten Commandments ([Exodus 20:2–17](https://ref.ly/Exod20:2-Exod20:17)). Noah ended the pre\-flood history, and Abram began a new era.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 5:1-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Enosh (ID: `person:Enosh`); Enoch (ID: `person:Enoch.2`); Lamech (ID: `person:Lamech.2`); Jared (ID: `person:Jared`); Mahalalel (ID: `person:Mahalalel`); Methuselah (ID: `person:Methuselah`); Adam (ID: `person:Adam`); Kenan (ID: `person:Kenan`); Seth (ID: `person:Seth`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Noah (ID: `person:Noah`); Japheth (ID: `person:Japheth`); Likeness (ID: `keyterm:Semblance`); Shem (ID: `person:Shem`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); To Curse (ID: `keyterm:Curse`); Ham (ID: `person:Ham`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Genesis 5:3 (id: 12926)

*in his own likeness, after his own image:* The image and likeness of God continue to exist in all human beings even after sin entered the world (see note on [1:26](https://ref.ly/Gen1:26)). When Adam had children, they carried both the image of God and the effects of Adam's sin ([Romans 5:12–14](https://ref.ly/Rom5:12-Rom5:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 5:3

## Genesis 5:5 (id: 12927)

*he died:* Adam died (see [2:17](https://ref.ly/Gen2:17); [3:18–19](https://ref.ly/Gen3:18-Gen3:19)) and so did his descendants (see [Romans 5:12–14](https://ref.ly/Rom5:12-Rom5:14)). 

The Bible does not mention Cain's violent actions here (see [Genesis 4:8](https://ref.ly/Gen4:8), [15](https://ref.ly/Gen4:15), [23–24](https://ref.ly/Gen4:23-Gen4:24)). Important figures in Seth's family line live in hope (see [5:29](https://ref.ly/Gen5:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 5:5

## Genesis 5:6 (id: 12928)

*the father of*: This can also mean “ancestor of.” Hebrew family lists do not always list every generation.
