## Genesis 40:1–23 (id: 12787)

Joseph did not lose faith in God’s promises, as evidenced by his readiness to interpret the dreams of two prisoners. He was still convinced that God’s revelation in his own two dreams ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)) was true, and he had not abandoned hope that they would be fulfilled. When the fellow prisoners’ dreams were fulfilled exactly as Joseph said, this confirmed that his previous dreams were from God.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 40:1-23

## Genesis 41:1–4 (id: 12795)

Pharaoh's first dream was about cows. Cows often stood half\-submerged in the Nile River's reeds to escape the heat and flies. They would leave the water to find grass. 

The second group of cows worried Pharaoh because they were thin but were able to swallow the fat cows.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:1-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 41:1–46 (id: 12794)

God had used two dreams to identify Joseph as a leader among his brothers ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)). He used two dreams to test Joseph’s faith in prison ([40:5–14](https://ref.ly/Gen40:5-Gen40:14)). Now he would use two dreams to elevate Joseph from prison to preeminence. Joseph had repeatedly proven faithful in small matters; now he would be put in charge of great things.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:1-46

## Genesis 41:8 (id: 12812)

The magicians and wise men belonged to a guild of supposed experts in spiritual matters, including dreams and visions (cp. [Exod 8:18–19](https://ref.ly/Exod8:18-Exod8:19); [Dan 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Dan2:10-Dan2:11)), but they could not interpret these dreams. God used an Israelite slave to confound the wisdom of the world (cp. [Dan 2](https://ref.ly/Dan2:1-Dan2:49)). However powerful a nation becomes, it is still under God’s sovereign control ([Dan 2:20–23](https://ref.ly/Dan2:20-Dan2:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`)

## Genesis 41:9–13 (id: 12813)

The chief cup\-bearer finally remembered Joseph and testified that his interpretations were true.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Hebrews (ID: `group:Hebrews`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 41:14–15 (id: 12796)

* Pharaoh quickly summoned Joseph from prison to explain his dreams.
* Joseph shaved, following the Egyptian custom.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Genesis 41:16 (id: 12797)

Joseph knew that only God could explain Pharaoh’s dreams (compare[40:8](https://ref.ly/Gen40:8)). He was sure God would reveal their meaning, as the dreams had a purpose ([41:25](https://ref.ly/Gen41:25), [28](https://ref.ly/Gen41:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 41:17–24 (id: 12798)

Pharaoh recounted his dreams and testified that no human wisdom could interpret them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:17-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Nile River (ID: `place:Nile`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Genesis 41:25–32 (id: 12799)

Both dreams predicted that seven years of abundant crops would be followed by seven years of severe famine.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:25-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 41:32 (id: 12800)

The two similar dreams showed that*the matter has been decreed by God, and He will carry it out shortly.* This was like the dreams of the two prisoners that God quickly fulfilled ([40:5–23](https://ref.ly/Gen40:5-Gen40:23)). Joseph’s own two dreams ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)) were also about to come true ([41:37–46](https://ref.ly/Gen41:37-Gen41:46); [42:6–9](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Genesis 41:33–36 (id: 12801)

God’s revelation demanded a response—it was not given just to satisfy curiosity about the future. Joseph’s advice about planning and preparing showed that he was the kind of intelligent and wise man that Pharaoh needed ([41:37–40](https://ref.ly/Gen41:37-Gen41:40)). • Joseph instituted central planning and control with a supervisor, local managers, a 20 percent tax on grain, and a rationing system. Later wisdom literature (see study note on 37:2–50:26) teaches the principle of planning ahead rather than living just for the moment (see [Prov 6:6–8](https://ref.ly/Prov6:6-Prov6:8); [27:12](https://ref.ly/Prov27:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:33-36
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 37:2-50.26 (ID: `12724@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`)

## Genesis 41:37–40 (id: 12802)

Pharaoh recognized that Joseph was the man for the job; he had the spirit of God and was intelligent and wise. God showed his sovereign rule in Egypt; Israelites who later read the account could be confident that God would save them as he had promised.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:37-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Perceive (ID: `keyterm:Perceive`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 41:41–46 (id: 12803)

Joseph was made the acting ruler or manager of Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:41-46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); On (ID: `place:On`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Genesis 41:42 (id: 12804)

Pharaoh’s signet ring had a seal used for signing documents. The seal was impressed in soft clay, which hardened and left a permanent impression of the ruler’s signature, which carried his authority. Numerous seals of this type have been found in archaeological digs. • The linen clothing and gold chain signified Joseph’s new status as ruler.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:42

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Genesis 41:43–44 (id: 12805)

Pharaoh made Joseph second\-in\-command; all the people had to submit to him. Compare [Psalm 105:16–22](https://ref.ly/Ps105:16-Ps105:22).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:43-44

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`)

## Genesis 41:45 (id: 12806)

As token of Joseph’s new status, Pharaoh gave him an Egyptian name and a wife from a high\-ranking family. • On was a center for sun worship that came to be known as Heliopolis (“sun city”).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); On (ID: `place:On`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`)

## Genesis 41:46 (id: 12807)

He was thirty years old: It had been approximately thirteen years since his brothers had sold Joseph into slavery ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2)). • he inspected the entire land of Egypt: As a wise manager, his first priority was to learn the scope of his responsibilities.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:46

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 41:47–57 (id: 12808)

Pharaoh's dreams were fulfilled just as Joseph had explained.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:47-57

## Genesis 41:50–52 (id: 12810)

In spite of his position and authority, Joseph never abandoned his heritage; he gave Hebrew names to his two sons. • Manasseh: Joseph’s prosperity and success made him “forget” the misery of separation from his family. • Ephraim: In so naming him, Joseph proclaimed his gratitude to God for making him “fruitful” in Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:50-52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Potiphera (ID: `person:Potiphera`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Asenath (ID: `person:Asenath`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); On (ID: `place:On`)

## Genesis 41:53–57 (id: 12811)

Joseph’s wisdom paid off, for the seven years of plenty were followed by seven years of severe famine, but throughout Egypt there was plenty of food. Joseph had grain to sell to the Egyptians and to people from other countries as well.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 41:53-57

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`)

## Genesis 42:1–44.34 (id: 12814)

Joseph did several unusual things to his brothers when they came looking for grain. The last time Joseph had been with them, they were filled with jealousy, hatred, and anger; they attempted to destroy their brother, and they deceived their father. Joseph put them through various tests, similar to the trying situations they had put him through, to see if they had changed.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:1-44:34

## Genesis 42:4 (id: 12829)

Jacob may have believed that Benjamin would not be safe with his brothers.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 42:6–7 (id: 12830)

Joseph’s first dream ([37:5–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:11)) was partially fulfilled when his brothers bowed down to him without recognizing him (see also [43:26](https://ref.ly/Gen43:26); [44:14](https://ref.ly/Gen44:14)). It was totally fulfilled in [50:18](https://ref.ly/Gen50:18). Joseph recognized his brothers immediately, but he could not reveal himself because he did not yet trust them to be the honest men they claimed to be ([42:10](https://ref.ly/Gen42:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`)

## Genesis 42:8 (id: 12831)

they didn’t recognize him: Joseph was a grown man, not a boy. He was not wearing a beard, was dressed in Egyptian clothes, and was in an unexpected position, speaking to them through an interpreter ([42:23](https://ref.ly/Gen42:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:9 (id: 12832)

You are spies! The brothers had considered Joseph a spy for their father and had treated him roughly ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen37:14), [18–28](https://ref.ly/Gen37:18-Gen37:28)). Joseph was putting them in a similar situation to see how they would respond.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:11 (id: 12816)

Joseph knew that they had not always been the honest men they claimed to be.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:11


## Genesis 42:15–17 (id: 12817)

Joseph put the brothers in jail for three days to see if they had a conscience functioning about what they had done ([42:21–23](https://ref.ly/Gen42:21-Gen42:23)). The brothers had similarly thrown Joseph into a cistern\-prison while they decided what to do with him ([37:24](https://ref.ly/Gen37:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:15-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Genesis 42:18–20 (id: 12818)

Rather than keep all but one, Joseph would release all but one to take grain home to their starving families.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:18-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Well-Established (ID: `keyterm:BeFirm`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 42:21–23 (id: 12819)

Clearly we are being punished: The brothers sensed that having to bring Benjamin back to Egypt against their father’s wishes was God’s punishment for their having sold Joseph to the traders. The sense of divine retribution began to awaken feelings of remorse that Joseph’s cries for mercy and their father’s tears ([37:34–35](https://ref.ly/Gen37:34-Gen37:35)) had failed to arouse.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:21-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); To Beg for Mercy (ID: `keyterm:BegForMercy`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:22 (id: 12820)

*you would not listen:* Reuben had lost his place as leader (see study notes on 42:37; 49:3–4).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:22
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:37 (ID: `12827@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 49:3-4 (ID: `12914@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Genesis 42:24 (id: 12821)

Joseph turned away from them and began to weep (cp. [43:30](https://ref.ly/Gen43:30); [45:2](https://ref.ly/Gen45:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Gen45:14); [50:1](https://ref.ly/Gen50:1), [17](https://ref.ly/Gen50:17)); perhaps he was hearing part of the story that he had never known (Reuben’s attempt to save him, [37:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen37:21-Gen37:22), [29](https://ref.ly/Gen37:29)), or his brothers’ remorse moved him to forgiveness.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`)

## Genesis 42:25 (id: 12823)

return each brother’s payment: He was now testing them to awaken their conscience and make them face their past guilt; once again, they were going home with silver instead of a brother ([37:28–35](https://ref.ly/Gen37:28-Gen37:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 42:25–28 (id: 12822)

Joseph cared for his brothers’ needs; he had forgiven them (see study note on 42:24) and was fulfilling his role to provide for them. God used Joseph’s care to convict the brothers even more fully of their sin.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:25-28
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:24 (ID: `12821@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 42:28 (id: 12824)

What has God done to us? They knew that God was behind everything that had been happening, so they faced a day of reckoning for their sins.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 42:29–34 (id: 12825)

The brothers’ account focused on the accusation that they were spies and on the need to take Benjamin back to Egypt with them. They omitted their growing realization of divine retribution for their crime against Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:29-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 42:36 (id: 12826)

Filled with grief over two sons lost already, Jacob feared that he would also lose Benjamin if he went to Egypt. • You are robbing me of my children! He did not realize the full truth of his words, but they must have stung his sons’ guilty consciences.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 42:37 (id: 12827)

Reuben tried to take the lead; perhaps he thought he could get back into his father’s good favor (see [35:22](https://ref.ly/Gen35:22)), first by rescuing Joseph from certain death ([37:21–22](https://ref.ly/Gen37:21-Gen37:22), [29–30](https://ref.ly/Gen37:29-Gen37:30)) and now by keeping Benjamin safe.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`)

## Genesis 42:38 (id: 12828)

Jacob was resolute in his favoritism toward Rachel’s remaining son. Benjamin would not go to Egypt even if it meant that Leah’s son Simeon never returned. Jacob’s grief apparently weighed heavily on the brothers’ conscience ([44:18–34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:18-Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 42:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`)

## Genesis 43:1–7 (id: 12833)

As the famine continued, Jacob’s family needed more grain, but they could not return to Egypt without Benjamin ([42:16](https://ref.ly/Gen42:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Gen42:20)). Jacob realized that he was in a bind; he needed more food but was loath to lose Benjamin.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:1-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 43:8–10 (id: 12845)

Judah broke the deadlock by taking responsibility for Benjamin’s well\-being, thus succeeding where Reuben failed. His action was fitting, since it had been his idea to sell Joseph to the Ishmaelites ([37:26–27](https://ref.ly/Gen37:26-Gen37:27)) instead of killing him. Now he would secure Benjamin’s safety with his own life (see [44:18–34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:18-Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:8-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 43:11–13 (id: 12834)

Jacob provided everything he could to ensure a favorable reception from the Egyptian governor ("the man"; compare [32:13–21](https://ref.ly/Gen32:13-Gen32:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pistachio Nuts (ID: `flora:PistachioNuts`); Almond (ID: `flora:Almond`); Rock Rose (ID: `flora:Ladanum`); Gum Tragacanth (ID: `flora:GumTragacanth`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Liquidambar (ID: `flora:Liquidambar`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Terebinth (ID: `flora:Terebinth`)

Jacob finally trusted *God Almighty* with his family's future. This divine title emphasizes God's power (see also [17:1](https://ref.ly/Gen17:1); [28:3](https://ref.ly/Gen28:3); [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11); [48:3](https://ref.ly/Gen48:3); [1 Kings 19:10](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:10), [14](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:14); [Revelation 21:22](https://ref.ly/Rev21:22)).

*When Joseph saw Benjamin with his brothers:* Joseph knew Benjamin was well (see study note on 42:1–44:34). The feast was both a celebration and a test.

* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 42:1-44.34 (ID: `12814@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 43:18 (id: 12837)

*the brothers were frightened:* Compare [32:6–12](https://ref.ly/Gen32:6-Gen32:12). Their guilt stopped them from seeing that something good might happen.

Joseph no longer treated his brothers harshly, but provided kind hospitality.

## Genesis 43:30 (id: 12842)

he was overcome with emotion for his brother: Cp. [42:24](https://ref.ly/Gen42:24). Joseph’s tears were of painful memories and years of lost fellowship, as well as of joy and thanksgiving at seeing his brother again. • He went into his private room: He did not yet plan to reveal his identity.

## Genesis 43:33 (id: 12843)

*in astonishment:* This ruler knew more about the brothers than seemed possible (compare [44:15](https://ref.ly/Gen44:15)).

## Genesis 43:34 (id: 12844)

The brothers experienced generous and gracious treatment from God through Joseph.

Joseph tested them. He showed special favor to Benjamin, who was also Rachel’s son. In the past, their father’s favoritism toward Joseph had caused jealousy and hatred.

By treating Benjamin with favor, Joseph tested whether they would respond with the same anger as before. This situation revealed whether their attitudes and behavior had changed.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 43:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 44:1–34 (id: 12846)

The brothers seemed different. They felt sorry for what they did to Joseph. They showed honor by returning the money and by bringing Benjamin. Given a chance to get rid of Rachel’s other son, Benjamin, would they do it?

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:1-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Peace (ID: `keyterm:IntactState`); Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`)

## Genesis 44:2 (id: 12852)

Joseph was giving his brothers the chance to abandon Benjamin if they wanted to. Joseph was testing them to see if they were loyal to the family and faithful to their father.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`)

## Genesis 44:5 (id: 12854)

This description would make the brothers understand that the ruler knew things that others could not. •*Hydromancy* (pouring water into oil) and *oenomancy* (pouring wine into other liquids) were methods of divination used in the ancient Near East that would have required such a cup. Joseph was continuing his ruse (see [42:7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:7); cp. [30:27](https://ref.ly/Gen30:27); see also [Lev 19:26](https://ref.ly/Lev19:26); [Num 23:23](https://ref.ly/Num23:23); [Deut 18:10–11](https://ref.ly/Deut18:10-Deut18:11))—he knew that only God grants revelation (see [Gen 37:5–9](https://ref.ly/Gen37:5-Gen37:9); [40:8](https://ref.ly/Gen40:8); [41:16](https://ref.ly/Gen41:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:5


## Genesis 44:9–10 (id: 12855)

The punishment that the brothers proposed was harsher than necessary; it was normal in antiquity to stake your life on what you said. The palace manager was agreeable but enforced a punishment that matched Joseph’s intentions ([44:17](https://ref.ly/Gen44:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Exempt (ID: `keyterm:Exempt`)

## Genesis 44:11–12 (id: 12847)

The palace manager knew where the cup was, but he created more anxiety by beginning with the oldest.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 44:13 (id: 12848)

*they all tore their clothes:* They knew what it would do to Jacob to lose Benjamin ([42:38](https://ref.ly/Gen42:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

## Genesis 44:14 (id: 12849)

This time, the brothers did not bow politely (see [42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:7); [43:26](https://ref.ly/Gen43:26)); they fell to the ground in desperation, fulfilling Joseph’s first dream for the third time (see [37:10](https://ref.ly/Gen37:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 44:16 (id: 12850)

Judah again spoke for the group. • God is punishing us for our sins: God was completing the work of repentance in their hearts. Judah again proposed that they all be punished. Benjamin was seemingly guilty of this theft, but all of the others were guilty of sin against Joseph. They preferred not returning to Jacob at all versus seeing his grief at the loss of Benjamin ([44:34](https://ref.ly/Gen44:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 44:18–34 (id: 12851)

Judah made good on his promise to pay for Benjamin’s safety ([43:8–10](https://ref.ly/Gen43:8-Gen43:10)). His lengthy plea to be imprisoned in place of the lad ([44:33](https://ref.ly/Gen44:33)) is one of the most moving acts of intercession in Scripture. It demonstrated Judah’s concern for their father and his willingness to give up everything for the sake of his brother. With this kind of integrity (see [John 15:13](https://ref.ly/John15:13)), Judah showed himself to be a true leader, qualified to receive the blessing of the firstborn. Through him the kings of Israel would come (see [Gen 49:10](https://ref.ly/Gen49:10)). • The brothers had fully repented, as expressed by Judah’s intercession. Because of their change, Joseph could make himself known to them ([45:1–15](https://ref.ly/Gen45:1-Gen45:15)) and arrange for the family to join him in Egypt where there was food ([45:16](https://ref.ly/Gen45:16); [47:12](https://ref.ly/Gen47:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:18-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Hell (ID: `place:Hell`); Love (ID: `keyterm:Love`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`)

## Genesis 44:32–34 (id: 12853)

Judah was willing to give up his family, his future, and his freedom for others.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 44:32-34

In one of the book's most dramatic scenes, Joseph told his brothers who he was. This brought the process of reconciling with them to a climax (see also [50:14–21](https://ref.ly/Gen50:14-Gen50:21)).

## Genesis 45:2 (id: 12864)

This is the third time Joseph cried over his brothers in the story (see also [42:24](https://ref.ly/Gen42:24); [43:30](https://ref.ly/Gen43:30); [45:14](https://ref.ly/Gen45:14); [50:17](https://ref.ly/Gen50:17); compare [50:1](https://ref.ly/Gen50:1)).

## Genesis 45:3 (id: 12865)

The news shocked the brothers. They were unable to speak because of amazement and fear ([45:5](https://ref.ly/Gen45:5); compare [50:15](https://ref.ly/Gen50:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 45:5–8 (id: 12866)

God . . . sent me is the central message of the account of Jacob’s family ([37:2](https://ref.ly/Gen37:2)). As the Lord had told Abraham, he was leading the Israelites into Egypt ([15:13](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13)). God had sent Joseph to Egypt to prepare for his family’s rescue during the famine. In what has become a classic statement of God’s sovereignty, Joseph explained that God had been working through all of the circumstances and human acts to bring about his plan. The certainty of God’s will is the basis for forgiveness and reconciliation with those who do wrong, cause hurt, or bring harm. If people do not believe that God is sovereign, then they will blame others and retaliate. Those who are spiritual will trust that God is at work even through human wickedness (see also [Rom 8:28–30](https://ref.ly/Rom8:28-Rom8:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:5-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 45:9–13 (id: 12867)

Joseph instructed his brothers to inform Jacob. The whole family was to move to Egypt and live in Goshen, a fertile region in the Nile delta (see [47:1–12](https://ref.ly/Gen47:1-Gen47:12)). If they did not come to Egypt, they would not survive the five years of famine ahead.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Be Poor (ID: `keyterm:Be`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 45:10 (id: 12857)

The region of Goshen was in the northeast corner of Egypt, only a few days’ walk from Canaan. Jacob’s family stayed there because there was food and water for themselves and their flocks; later, they were kept there by Egyptians who put them to slave labor.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 45:14–15 (id: 12858)

Joseph was reunited with his brothers—first with Benjamin and then with the rest. Their previous hatred and jealousy of Joseph ([37:4–11](https://ref.ly/Gen37:4-Gen37:11)) had come to an end. The brothers experienced forgiveness from God and from Joseph. Far from commanding his brothers to bow down to him (see [42:6–7](https://ref.ly/Gen42:6-Gen42:7)), Joseph welcomed them. Joseph held no grudge because he accepted what had happened as God’s work and saw the good that had resulted (see [50:14–21](https://ref.ly/Gen50:14-Gen50:21)). This is how wisdom rules: The wise leader will forgive and restore (see study note on 37:2–50:26).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:14-15
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 37:2-50.26 (ID: `12724@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Benjamin (ID: `person:Benjamin`)

## Genesis 45:16–25 (id: 12859)

Joseph sent his brothers with instructions to bring Jacob and the entire family to Egypt. Out of gratitude for Joseph’s having saved all of Egypt ([45:18](https://ref.ly/Gen45:18); see [47:20](https://ref.ly/Gen47:20)), Pharaoh promised the best of all the land of Egypt, and Joseph gave them the finest products of Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 45:16-25

As expected, Jacob was shocked when he heard that his son Joseph was still alive and ruling all Egypt. But as he heard their full report and saw everything Joseph had sent, he became convinced it was true. At once, he prepared to go to Egypt and see Joseph again after 22 years.

## Genesis 45:27 (id: 12863)

their father’s spirits revived: This royal invitation to Jacob, an old man near the end of hope, and to the ten brothers burdened with guilty fears, was a turning point in their lives. It was also a fulfillment of God’s prediction ([15:13–16](https://ref.ly/Gen15:13-Gen15:16)) that they would go into seclusion in a foreign country and there become a great nation without losing their identity. The joyful news about Joseph changed the lives of everyone in this family for the good.

## Genesis 46:1 (id: 12869)

set out for Egypt: A little over 200 years earlier, Abraham had similarly gone down into Egypt during a famine in Canaan ([12:10](https://ref.ly/Gen12:10)), and God had protected him there. • Jacob’s first stop was at Beersheba, where Abraham had sacrificed to the Lord and worshiped him after settling his land and water rights with the Philistines ([21:31–33](https://ref.ly/Gen21:31-Gen21:33)). This was where Isaac had lived, and where Jacob had lived before he fled from Esau’s anger ([28:10](https://ref.ly/Gen28:10)). • all his possessions: See [46:5–7](https://ref.ly/Gen46:5-Gen46:7).

[12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

## Genesis 46:30 (id: 12876)

Jacob was satisfied just to see his beloved son alive—the firstborn of his chosen wife Rachel and the designated family leader (see study note on 48:5–7; see also [1 Chr 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). More than just a family reunion, this was confirmation that God’s plan was intact.

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 46:34 (id: 12877)

In contrast to the syncretistic Canaanites, who would have absorbed the Israelites had they stayed in Canaan, Egyptians detested Semitic shepherds out of a sense of ethnic superiority and observed a strict segregation (see [43:32](https://ref.ly/Gen43:32)). When Jacob’s family settled in Egypt, this separation would allow the people to grow into a great nation without losing their identity.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 46:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Image (ID: `realia:Image`)

## Genesis 47:1 (id: 12882)

Goshen (see study note on 45:10) is not referred to in ancient Egyptian texts; the name it bore in later Egyptian writings was “the region of Rameses” ([47:11](https://ref.ly/Gen47:11); see [Exod 1:11](https://ref.ly/Exod1:11)). It was fertile and near to Joseph at court, which suggests that it was on the eastern side of the Nile delta.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:1
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 45:10 (ID: `12857@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 47:1–6 (id: 12880)

Pharaoh responded as Joseph hoped by giving Jacob’s family the best part of the land; he even gave some of the brothers oversight of his own livestock ([47:6](https://ref.ly/Gen47:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:1-6

## Genesis 47:7–10 (id: 12887)

When Jacob entered Pharaoh’s court and when he left, he blessed Pharaoh, an indication of Jacob’s position as God’s representative (see [Heb 7:7](https://ref.ly/Heb7:7)). God had promised that he would bless those who blessed Abraham’s family ([Gen 12:2–3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:2-Gen12:3)), so he now blessed Pharaoh and Egypt.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:7-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 47:13–26 (id: 12881)

The Lord blessed Pharaoh because Pharaoh was blessing Abraham’s descendants ([12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)). Through Joseph’s wise administration in Egypt, the Lord saved the people from starvation and prospered Pharaoh. In selling food to the Egyptians during the years of famine, Joseph accepted money, livestock, and finally land as payment, until almost all of Egypt belonged to Pharaoh. Meanwhile, God provided Israel with some of the best land in Egypt where they could live, work, and multiply.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:13-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Complete (ID: `keyterm:Complete`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Horse (ID: `fauna:Horse`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 47:21 (id: 12883)

he made them all slaves: In Hebrew script, the difference is very slight between *slaves* (Hebrew ‘abadim) and *towns* (Hebrew ‘arim). Moving the people into the towns doesn’t fit the context very well, so most translations select *slaves* as the reading that makes the most sense (cp. [47:20](https://ref.ly/Gen47:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 47:27 (id: 12884)

God blessed his people according to his promise to Abraham that his descendants would be innumerable ([15:5](https://ref.ly/Gen15:5); [22:17](https://ref.ly/Gen22:17)). They had to wait for the fulfillment of the second promise, that they would own the land of Canaan ([17:8](https://ref.ly/Gen17:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 47:29–31 (id: 12885)

bury me with my ancestors: Cp. [49:29–33](https://ref.ly/Gen49:29-Gen49:33). Jacob wanted to be buried with Abraham and Isaac in the cave of Machpelah (see study note on 49:29–33; see [23:1–20](https://ref.ly/Gen23:1-Gen23:20); [25:7–10](https://ref.ly/Gen25:7-Gen25:10); [35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29)). • Put your hand under my thigh: This custom (cp. [24:1–9](https://ref.ly/Gen24:1-Gen24:9) and study note on 24:2) was a serious oath to carry on the covenant, which had as its main promise innumerable descendants in the Promised Land.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:29-31
* **Associated Articles:** Genesis 24:2 (ID: `12490@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Genesis 49:29-33 (ID: `12913@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Genesis 47:31 (id: 12886)

When the oath was taken, Jacob bowed humbly in worship and thanked the Lord for ensuring that he would be buried with his ancestors in the land of promise (cp. [1 Kgs 1:47](https://ref.ly/1Kgs1:47)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 47:31

## Genesis 48:1–22 (id: 12888)

In blessing Ephraim and Manasseh, Jacob reached out by faith for the promise to be continued, having learned that God’s ways are not always the ways of men. Out of Jacob’s long life, the writer to the Hebrews selected the blessing of Joseph’s sons as his great act of faith ([Heb 11:21](https://ref.ly/Heb11:21)). As Jacob acted in light of God’s will, the primary blessing was again given to the younger instead of the older son, but without scheming and its bitter results.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:1-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Paddan-aram (ID: `place:Paddan-aram`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Bethel (ID: `place:Bethel`); Rachel (ID: `person:Rachel`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Bethlehem (ID: `place:Bethlehem`); To Cause to Possess (ID: `keyterm:Possess`); Simeon (ID: `person:Simeon.5`); Reuben (ID: `person:Reuben`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

Jacob rehearsed how God Almighty had appeared to him and had promised him Abraham’s blessing—innumerable descendants dwelling in the land . . . as an everlasting possession (cp. [28:10–22](https://ref.ly/Gen28:10-Gen28:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:3-4

## Genesis 48:5–7 (id: 12895)

Jacob, prompted by his memory of Rachel (see [35:16–20](https://ref.ly/Gen35:16-Gen35:20)), blessed Joseph by elevating his two sons as coheirs with his other sons—the tribes of Ephraim and Manasseh would have shares along with the other tribes that came from Jacob (see [Josh 16–17](https://ref.ly/Josh16:1-Josh17:18)). Jacob also gave Ephraim, Joseph’s younger son, the birthright (see [1 Chr 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). As a result of this blessing, Ephraim and Manasseh became large and powerful tribes (see [Josh 17:14–18](https://ref.ly/Josh17:14-Josh17:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:5-7

## Genesis 48:10 (id: 12889)

Like his father Isaac, Jacob now gave the blessing when his eyesight was failing (compare [27:1](https://ref.ly/Gen27:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 48:14 (id: 12890)

The right hand was for the head of the firstborn, and Jacob was deliberately giving that position to the younger son. That pattern was followed for four consecutive generations: Isaac over Ishmael, Jacob over Esau, Joseph over Reuben, and Ephraim over Manasseh. Many years later, Ephraim became the leading tribe in the northern kingdom, superior to the tribe of Manasseh. The entire northern kingdom of Israel was occasionally called Ephraim (see study notes on 2 Chr 28:12; Isa 11:13; Ezek 37:15–28; Hos 6:4; Zech 9:10).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:14
* **Associated Articles:** Ezekiel 37:15-28 (ID: `11764@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Hosea 6:4 (ID: `13462@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Isaiah 11:13 (ID: `15841@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Zechariah 9:10 (ID: `26133@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 48:15–16 (id: 12891)

In his blessing on Joseph, Jacob used a threefold invocation to describe the God in whom he trusted: (1\) the God who was in covenant with his fathers Abraham and Isaac ([28:13](https://ref.ly/Gen28:13); [31:5](https://ref.ly/Gen31:5), [42](https://ref.ly/Gen31:42); [32:9](https://ref.ly/Gen32:9); [46:3](https://ref.ly/Gen46:3)); (2\) the God who had been his shepherd (cp. [49:24](https://ref.ly/Gen49:24); [Exod 6:6](https://ref.ly/Exod6:6); [Ps 23:1](https://ref.ly/Ps23:1); [Isa 59:20](https://ref.ly/Isa59:20)); and (3\) the Angel who rescued him from all harm. He prayed the same blessings for Joseph’s sons.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`)

## Genesis 48:17–19 (id: 12892)

Joseph was upset: He expected God to act according to convention, but faith recognizes that God’s ways are not man’s ways, and God’s thoughts are not man’s thoughts. It took Jacob a lifetime to learn this lesson, but he did learn it, and here he acted on it.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:17-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

Joseph was later buried at Shechem ([Josh 24:32](https://ref.ly/Josh24:32)) as a sign that he possessed this bequeathed extra portion (Hebrew shekem) of land. Jacob had apparently conquered this area though the occasion is not mentioned elsewhere.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 48:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Bow (ID: `realia:Bow`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Genesis 49:1–2 (id: 12897)

Jacob’s words were deliberately chosen prophetic oracles. The days to come refer to the conquest and settlement of the Promised Land, and beyond that to the messianic age. They would all share in the blessing; all the tribes would enter the land with Joshua, but they would not all participate equally.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 49:1–28 (id: 12896)

Jacob, by faith and as God’s spokesman, looked forward to Israel’s settlement in the land, and beyond that to the glorious future. Here at the end of the patriarchal age, he foretold what would happen to each tribe as he evaluated his sons one by one, just as Noah had done at the end of the primeval era (cp. [9:25–27](https://ref.ly/Gen9:25-Gen9:27)). The character and acts of each ancestor affected the lives of his descendants ([Exod 20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6); [34:6–7](https://ref.ly/Exod34:6-Exod34:7); [Num 14:18](https://ref.ly/Num14:18); [Jer 32:18](https://ref.ly/Jer32:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:1-28

[Judg 5:15–16](https://ref.ly/Judg5:15-Judg5:16); [1 Chr 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)).

## Genesis 49:5–7 (id: 12916)

Simeon and Levi were violent and lawless; instead of serving justice, they indulged their uncontrolled anger and disregarded life ([34:24–29](https://ref.ly/Gen34:24-Gen34:29)). • I will scatter them: Simeon’s land was largely absorbed into Judah’s ([Josh 19:1](https://ref.ly/Josh19:1), [9](https://ref.ly/Josh19:9)); Levi was given a more honorable future because the Levites became the priestly tribe (see [Exod 32:25–29](https://ref.ly/Exod32:25-Exod32:29)), but they had no region of their own ([Josh 21](https://ref.ly/Josh21:1-Josh21:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:5-7

## Genesis 49:8–12 (id: 12917)

The blessing on Judah commands the most attention. In this oracle, Jacob predicted the fierce, lion\-like dominance of Judah over his enemies and over his brothers, who would praise him (cp. [29:35](https://ref.ly/Gen29:35); see, e.g., [Ruth 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Ruth4:11-Ruth4:12); [1 Sam 18:6–7](https://ref.ly/1Sam18:6-1Sam18:7); [Pss 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps45:17), [72](https://ref.ly/Ps72:1-Ps72:20); [Isa 11:1–13](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:13)).

## Genesis 49:10 (id: 12898)

This verse anticipates the kingship in Judah (cp. [17:6](https://ref.ly/Gen17:6), [16](https://ref.ly/Gen17:16); [35:11](https://ref.ly/Gen35:11)). Although the birthright blessing went to Joseph, Judah would provide Israel’s rulers (see [1 Chr 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). A long line of kings from Judah would retain the scepter, the symbol of rule; the last king would be the one to whom it belongs, the promised Messiah (see [2 Sam 7:4–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:4-2Sam7:16); [Pss 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12), [45](https://ref.ly/Ps45:1-Ps45:17), [60](https://ref.ly/Ps60:1-Ps60:12); [Isa 11](https://ref.ly/Isa11:1-Isa11:16); [Ezek 21:26–27](https://ref.ly/Ezek21:26-Ezek21:27); [Zech 9:9](https://ref.ly/Zech9:9); [Rev 5:5](https://ref.ly/Rev5:5)). • from his descendants: Literally *from between his feet,* a poetic euphemism for reproductive organs. • until the coming of the one to whom it belongs (Or *until tribute is brought to him and the peoples obey;* traditionally rendered *until Shiloh comes*): These differences arise from ambiguities in the Hebrew text. Rule of Israel belongs to Judah’s descendant through David’s line ([2 Sam 7:8–16](https://ref.ly/2Sam7:8-2Sam7:16)), and he will eventually rule all nations, as signified by the bringing of *tribute* (see [Pss 68:29](https://ref.ly/Ps68:29); [72:8–11](https://ref.ly/Ps72:8-Ps72:11); [Isa 2:2–4](https://ref.ly/Isa2:2-Isa2:4); [Eph 4:8–10](https://ref.ly/Eph4:8-Eph4:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Shiloh (ID: `person:Shiloh`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Rod (ID: `realia:Rod`); Staff (ID: `realia:Staff`)

## Genesis 49:11–12 (id: 12899)

These descriptions envision the abundance of the Messiah’s kingdom (see [Isa 61:6–7](https://ref.ly/Isa61:6-Isa61:7); [65:21–25](https://ref.ly/Isa65:21-Isa65:25); [Zech 3:10](https://ref.ly/Zech3:10)). When the Messiah comes, there will be paradise\-like splendor and abundance on the earth. • He ties his foal to a grapevine: Grapevines will be so abundant that they will be used for hitching posts, and wine will be as abundant as fresh water (see [Amos 9:13–14](https://ref.ly/Amos9:13-Amos9:14); [Zech 3:10](https://ref.ly/Zech3:10)). • The coming one will have eyes . . . darker than wine and teeth . . . whiter than milk: He will be vigorous and healthy, as will be the era of his rule. Jesus’ miracle of changing water into wine ([John 2:1–12](https://ref.ly/John2:1-John2:12)), his first sign, was an announcement that the Messiah had come; it was a foretaste of even better things to come.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Donkey (ID: `fauna:Donkey`)

The oracle said Zebulun would dwell by the sea and be a safe harbor, but in the actual settlement they spread inland (see [Josh 19:10–16](https://ref.ly/Josh19:10-Josh19:16)). The oracle did not give specific borders for the tribes.

## Genesis 49:18 (id: 12903)

At this point, Jacob paused to speak words of hope. He may have been reminding his sons of their need to depend on the Lord. Or he may have been expressing hope in the Messiah’s reign, when he and his descendants would be rescued from all trouble, grief, and human betrayal.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:18

## Genesis 49:19 (id: 12904)

Three of the six Hebrew words in this verse are wordplays on the name Gad (“attack”). Gad will be attacked by marauding bands (attackers), but he will attack. The tribes that settled east of the Jordan River frequently experienced border raids (see [Josh 13](https://ref.ly/Josh13:1-Josh13:33); [2 Kgs 10:32–33](https://ref.ly/2Kgs10:32-2Kgs10:33); [1 Chr 5:18–19](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:18-1Chr5:19)).

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gad (ID: `person:Gad`)

## Genesis 49:21 (id: 12906)

Naphtali was like a doe. This may mean the tribe would live as a free people in the mountains (compare [Judges 5:18](https://ref.ly/Judg5:18)). The tribe of Naphtali settled in the hill country northwest of the Sea of Galilee.

## Genesis 49:22–26 (id: 12907)

This oracle treats Joseph more expansively than any of the others, for here the main blessing lay (see [1 Chr 5:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Chr5:1-1Chr5:2)). Jacob lavished promises of victory and prosperity on Joseph’s two tribes. Ephraimites recorded as victorious in battle include Joshua ([Josh 6](https://ref.ly/Josh6:1-Josh6:27), [8](https://ref.ly/Josh8:1-Josh8:35), [10](https://ref.ly/Josh10:1-Josh10:43), [12](https://ref.ly/Josh12:1-Josh12:24)) and Deborah ([Judg 4](https://ref.ly/Judg4:1-Judg4:24)). Victorious descendants of Manasseh include Gideon ([Judg 6–8](https://ref.ly/Judg6:1-Judg8:35)) and Jephthah ([Judg 11:1–12:7](https://ref.ly/Judg11:1-Judg12:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:22-26

## Genesis 49:28 (id: 12912)

These prophecies cover a wide range, predicting the future of various tribes in general terms. Individuals could receive great blessings from God through faith and obedience, no matter what happened to their clan.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 49:29–33 (id: 12913)

Bury me with my father: This grave in the land of Canaan represented hope for the future (cp. [47:29–30](https://ref.ly/Gen47:29-Gen47:30)). Others buried at the cave of Machpelah near Hebron were Sarah ([23:19](https://ref.ly/Gen23:19)), Abraham ([25:7–9](https://ref.ly/Gen25:7-Gen25:9)), Isaac ([35:27–29](https://ref.ly/Gen35:27-Gen35:29)), Rebekah, and Leah.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:29-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Rebekah (ID: `person:Rebekah`); Leah (ID: `person:Leah`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Sarah (ID: `person:Sarah`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Genesis 49:33 (id: 12915)

Jacob died at the age of 147 ([47:28](https://ref.ly/Gen47:28)), bringing his life of struggle and sorrow to an end. Jacob had always had an unquenchable desire for God’s blessing. He had a deep piety that habitually relied on God despite all else. In the end, he died a man of genuine faith. He learned where real blessings come from, and through his faith would be able to hand these on to his sons ([Heb 11:21](https://ref.ly/Heb11:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 49:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Genesis 50:1–6 (id: 12929)

Like his father and his grandfather, Jacob’s death marked the end of an important time in Israel’s history.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 50:2 (id: 12937)

Jacob’s body was embalmed for burial in typical Egyptian fashion. Embalming is the process of preserving a body after death.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Genesis 50:3 (id: 12938)

The Egyptians mourned Jacob for 70 days, just two days less than they would for a pharaoh. This showed their great respect for Joseph.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`)

## Genesis 50:4–6 (id: 12939)

Joseph needed Pharaoh’s permission to leave his official duties for a short time. He wanted to bury his father in Canaan.

Pharaoh quickly gave permission to Joseph, who had once been a slave.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Genesis 50:7–9 (id: 12940)

This was Joseph’s first return to his homeland in thirty\-nine years. The trip was temporary. Centuries later, the family of Israel would permanently leave Egypt, taking Joseph’s bones with them for burial in the land of promise (see [50:25](https://ref.ly/Gen50:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:7-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Goshen (ID: `place:Goshen`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Pharaoh (ID: `person:Pharaoh.4`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Chariot (ID: `realia:Chariot`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Genesis 50:10–13 (id: 12930)

This journey to Canaan was a sorrowful one, because they went there to bury a man. The next journey into the land would be to live there.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Atad (ID: `place:Atad`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Ephron (ID: `person:Ephron.2`); Machpelah (ID: `place:Machpelah`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Mamre (ID: `place:Mamre`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`)

## Genesis 50:15–18 (id: 12931)

The brothers begged Joseph to forgive them, referring to themselves as his slaves (compare [37:7](https://ref.ly/Gen37:7); [44:16](https://ref.ly/Gen44:16), [33](https://ref.ly/Gen44:33)). They worried that Joseph had only made peace with them earlier because he wanted to see his father again. Now that Jacob and Pharaoh were not there to restrain him, they feared he might seek revenge. But *Joseph wept* because they still feared he would punish them.

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:15-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`)

## Genesis 50:19–21 (id: 12932)

Joseph comforted his brothers, explaining that God intended to fulfill the promised blessing (compare [45:5](https://ref.ly/Gen45:5), [7–9](https://ref.ly/Gen45:7-Gen45:9)). He also promised them kindness and support (see [45:11](https://ref.ly/Gen45:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:19-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`)

## Genesis 50:22–23 (id: 12933)

Joseph lived to see his great\-great\-grandchildren by Ephraim, and his great\-grandchildren by Manasseh. This was a sign of God's blessing (see [Psalm 128:6](https://ref.ly/Ps128:6); [Proverbs 17:6](https://ref.ly/Prov17:6); [Isaiah 53:10](https://ref.ly/Isa53:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:22-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Machir (ID: `person:Machir`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Genesis 50:23 (id: 12934)

*were brought up on Joseph’s knees**:* Placing them on his knees at their birth was a symbolic act signifying that they came from him and belonged to him (compare [Job 3:12](https://ref.ly/Job3:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Machir (ID: `person:Machir`); Manasseh (ID: `person:Manasseh`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Ephraim (ID: `person:Ephraim`)

## Genesis 50:24–25 (id: 12935)

God will surely come to help you (literally *visit you*): These words of Joseph, given twice, summarize the hope expressed throughout both the Old Testament and the New Testament. God’s visitation in the person of the Messiah, the offspring of Abraham, would bring the curse to an end and establish the long\-awaited blessing of God in a new creation. The company of the faithful would wait in expectation for that to happen. • Like his father before him, Joseph made his brothers promise that his bones would be taken out of Egypt when God would come to take them (to help you and lead you back) to Canaan (see [Exod 13:19](https://ref.ly/Exod13:19); [Josh 24:32](https://ref.ly/Josh24:32); [Heb 11:22](https://ref.ly/Heb11:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:24-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.10`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`)

## Genesis 50:26 (id: 12936)

Joseph’s death signified the end of his generation (see study notes on 25:7–8; 35:1–29) and of the patriarchal age. From this point forward, God dealt with Israel as a nation. • Joseph’s body was kept in Egypt as a pledge of hope for slaves awaiting the Promised Land (see [Exod 13:19](https://ref.ly/Exod13:19); [Heb 11:39–40](https://ref.ly/Heb11:39-Heb11:40)). He was eventually buried in Shechem (see [Josh 24:32](https://ref.ly/Josh24:32)), where Jacob had originally sent him ([Gen 37:13](https://ref.ly/Gen37:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Genesis 50:26
